# Third-party notices

oIDE is MIT licensed (see [LICENSE](LICENSE)). Packaged builds of oIDE include the third-party software
listed below. Each is used under the licence shown; the full licence and notice texts follow the table.

Electron ships its own notices for Chromium, Node.js and their dependencies as `LICENSES.chromium.html`
next to the oIDE executable.

This file is generated by `pnpm licenses:notices` (`tools/licenses/notices.mjs`); do not edit it by hand.
`pnpm licenses:check` fails CI when it is stale or when a shipped licence is not MIT-compatible.

400 packages, and 12 font families listed under [Bundled fonts](#bundled-fonts) at the end.

Native modules also ship one prebuilt binary package for the target OS and CPU. Those packages are
published from the same repository under the same licence as their parent and are not listed one by one:

- `@lydell/node-pty-<platform>` (MIT): `darwin-arm64`, `darwin-x64`, `linux-arm64`, `linux-x64`, `win32-arm64`, `win32-x64`
- `@parcel/watcher-<platform>` (MIT): `android-arm64`, `darwin-arm64`, `darwin-x64`, `freebsd-x64`, `linux-arm-glibc`, `linux-arm-musl`, `linux-arm64-glibc`, `linux-arm64-musl`, `linux-x64-glibc`, `linux-x64-musl`, `win32-arm64`, `win32-x64`

## argparse 2.0.1

Licence: Python-2.0 · nodeca/argparse

```text
A. HISTORY OF THE SOFTWARE
==========================

Python was created in the early 1990s by Guido van Rossum at Stichting
Mathematisch Centrum (CWI, see http://www.cwi.nl) in the Netherlands
as a successor of a language called ABC.  Guido remains Python's
principal author, although it includes many contributions from others.

In 1995, Guido continued his work on Python at the Corporation for
National Research Initiatives (CNRI, see http://www.cnri.reston.va.us)
in Reston, Virginia where he released several versions of the
software.

In May 2000, Guido and the Python core development team moved to
BeOpen.com to form the BeOpen PythonLabs team.  In October of the same
year, the PythonLabs team moved to Digital Creations, which became
Zope Corporation.  In 2001, the Python Software Foundation (PSF, see
https://www.python.org/psf/) was formed, a non-profit organization
created specifically to own Python-related Intellectual Property.
Zope Corporation was a sponsoring member of the PSF.

All Python releases are Open Source (see http://www.opensource.org for
the Open Source Definition).  Historically, most, but not all, Python
releases have also been GPL-compatible; the table below summarizes
the various releases.

    Release         Derived     Year        Owner       GPL-
                    from                                compatible? (1)

    0.9.0 thru 1.2              1991-1995   CWI         yes
    1.3 thru 1.5.2  1.2         1995-1999   CNRI        yes

Footnotes:

(1) GPL-compatible doesn't mean that we're distributing Python under
    the GPL.  All Python licenses, unlike the GPL, let you distribute
    a modified version without making your changes open source.  The
    GPL-compatible licenses make it possible to combine Python with
    other software that is released under the GPL; the others don't.

(2) According to Richard Stallman, 1.6.1 is not GPL-compatible,
    because its license has a choice of law clause.  According to
    CNRI, however, Stallman's lawyer has told CNRI's lawyer that 1.6.1
    is "not incompatible" with the GPL.
```

```text
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';

import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import { dirname } from 'path';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = dirname(__filename);

const year = (new Date()).getFullYear();

const license = `Copyright (c) 2016-${year}, The Cytoscape Consortium.

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of
this software and associated documentation files (the “Software”), to deal in
the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to
use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies
```

---

## Bundled fonts

Fonts are files, not code, so they are listed here by family. The Excalidraw fonts are copied from
`@excalidraw/excalidraw` into `excalidraw-assets/fonts/`; the others are emitted by the build from the
packages named. Licence texts follow the table; each family lists its copyright notices, including the
strings embedded in the shipped files.

| Font | Licence | From | Font files in source | Shipped as |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Assistant](https://github.com/hafontia/Assistant) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 4 | Excalidraw UI font (copied with the Excalidraw fonts, and emitted by Vite from its CSS) |
| [Cascadia Code](https://github.com/microsoft/cascadia-code) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 1 | Excalidraw "Code" font family |
| [Comic Shanns](https://github.com/jesusmgg/comic-shanns-mono) | MIT | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 4 | Excalidraw "Comic Shanns" font family |
| [Excalifont](https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/tree/master/packages/excalidraw/fonts/Excalifont) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 7 | Excalidraw default hand-drawn font |
| [Liberation Sans](https://github.com/liberationfonts/liberation-1.7-fonts) | GPL-2.0-only WITH Font-exception-2.0 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 1 | Excalidraw "Helvetica" fallback font |
| [Lilita One](https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Lilita+One) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 2 | Excalidraw "Lilita One" font family |
| [Nunito](https://github.com/googlefonts/nunito) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 5 | Excalidraw "Nunito" font family |
| [Virgil](https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/tree/master/packages/excalidraw/fonts/Virgil) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 1 | Excalidraw legacy hand-drawn font |
| [Xiaolai SC](https://github.com/lxgw/kose-font) | OFL-1.1 | `@excalidraw/excalidraw` | 209 | Excalidraw CJK fallback font (209 subset files) |
| [Inter](https://github.com/rsms/inter) | OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource/inter` | 252 | oIDE UI font (latin 400-700, emitted by Vite) |
| [JetBrains Mono](https://github.com/JetBrains/JetBrainsMono) | OFL-1.1 | `@fontsource/jetbrains-mono` | 192 | oIDE code and path font (latin 400-500, emitted by Vite) |
| [Codicons](https://github.com/microsoft/vscode-codicons) | CC-BY-4.0 | `monaco-editor` | 1 | Monaco editor icon font (emitted by Vite) |

Licence: GPL-2.0-only WITH Font-exception-2.0 · https://github.com/liberationfonts/liberation-1.7-fonts · from `@excalidraw/excalidraw/dist/prod/fonts/Liberation`

Licence verified from name table of the shipped file (Liberation Sans 1.05, "subject to the license agreement under which you accepted the Liberation font software") and the Liberation 1.x License.txt.

Liberation Sans 1.05 is under the GNU GPL v2 with the font exception (Red Hat's Liberation 1.x licence). oIDE ships it unmodified, as a separate file that Excalidraw loads for its "Helvetica" family; it is not linked into oIDE's code. Its source is the upstream Liberation 1.x font release (https://github.com/liberationfonts/liberation-1.7-fonts) and the identical file in the Excalidraw repository (https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/tree/master/packages/excalidraw/fonts/Liberation).

### Licence text: OFL-1.1

Applies to: Assistant, Cascadia Code, Excalifont, Lilita One, Nunito, Virgil, Xiaolai SC, Inter, JetBrains Mono.

```text

-----------------------------------------------------------

The goals of the Open Font License (OFL) are to stimulate worldwide
development of collaborative font projects, to support the font creation
efforts of academic and linguistic communities, and to provide a free and
open framework in which fonts may be shared and improved in partnership
with others.

redistributed and/or sold with any software provided that any reserved

include source files, build scripts and documentation.
```
